  court, and may be increased or reduced by the official receiver.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee to take possession and make
  inventory

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The trustee shall, as soon as possible, take
  possession of the deeds, books, records and documents and all property
  of the bankrupt and make an inventory, and for the purpose of making
  an inventory the trustee is entitled to enter, subject to subsection
  (3.1), on any premises on which the deeds, books, records, documents
  or property of the bankrupt may be, even if they are in the possession
  of an executing officer, a secured creditor or other claimant to them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warrant required to enter

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} Where the premises referred to in subsection (3)
  are occupied by a person other than the bankrupt, the trustee may not
  enter the premises without the consent of that other person except
  under the authority of a warrant issued under section 189.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee to be receiver

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The trustee shall, in relation to and for the purpose
  of acquiring or retaining possession of the property of the bankrupt,
  be in the same position as if he were a receiver of the property
  appointed by the court, and the court may on his application enforce
  the acquisition or retention accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of trustee to books of account,
  etc.

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No person is, as against the trustee, entitled to
  withhold possession of the books of account belonging to the bankrupt
  or any papers or documents, including material in electronic form,
  relating to the accounts or to any trade dealings of the bankrupt or
  to set up any lien or right of retention thereon.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 16
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 3
- 1994, c. 26, s. 7
- 1997, c. 12, s. 17
- 2004, c. 25, s. 18
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 16"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property to be delivered to trustee

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person has in his possession or power any property of the bankrupt
  that he is not by law entitled to retain as against the bankrupt or
  the trustee, that person shall deliver the property to the trustee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to act anywhere

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of obtaining possession of and
  realizing on the property of the bankrupt, a trustee has power to act
  as such anywhere.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. B-3, s. 12
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conservatory measures

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** The trustee may when
necessary in the interests of the estate of the bankrupt

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} take conservatory measures and summarily dispose of
  property that is perishable or likely to depreciate rapidly in value;
  and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} carry on the business of the bankrupt until the date
  fixed for the first meeting of creditors.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. B-3, s. 12
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Legal advice or action before first
meeting

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  trustee may prior to the first meeting of creditors obtain such legal
  advice and take such court proceedings as he may consider necessary
  for the recovery or protection of the property of the bankrupt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}In case of emergency

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of an emergency where the necessary
  authority cannot be obtained from the inspectors in time to take
  appropriate action, the trustee may obtain such legal advice and
  institute such legal proceedings and take such action as he may deem
  necessary in the interests of the estate of the bankrupt.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2005, c. 47, s. 18\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 19
- 2004, c. 25, s. 19(F)
- 2005, c. 47, s. 18
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19-20041215.html "Link to previous version of section 19"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Divesting property by trustee

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  trustee may, with the permission of the inspectors, divest all or any
  part of the trustee's right, title or interest in any real property or
  immovable of the bankrupt by a notice of quit claim or renunciation by
  the trustee, and the official in charge of the land titles or registry
  office, as the case may be, where title to the real property or
  immovable is registered shall accept and register in the land register
  the notice when tendered for registration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Registration of a notice under subsection (1)
  operates as a discharge or release of any documents previously
  registered in the land register by or on behalf of the trustee with
  respect to the property referred to in the notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 20
- 1997, c. 12, s. 18
- 2004, c. 25, s. 20
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-20-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 20"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Verifying bankrupt's statement of affairs

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** The trustee shall verify
the bankrupt's statement of affairs referred to in paragraph 158(d).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 21
- 2005, c. 47, s. 19
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-21-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties regarding returns

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** The trustee is not liable
to make any return that the bankrupt was required to make more than one
year prior to the commencement of the calendar year, or the fiscal year
of the bankrupt where that is different from the calendar year, in which
he became a bankrupt.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. B-3, s. 12
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee to permit inspection of records

**[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** The trustee shall at all
reasonable times permit any authorized person to inspect the books and
papers of the bankrupt in order to prepare or verify returns that the
bankrupt is by statute required to file.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. B-3, s. 12
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insuring property

- **[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  trustee shall forthwith temporarily insure and keep insured in his
  official name all the insurable property of the bankrupt, for such
  amount and against such hazards as he may deem advisable until the
  inspectors are appointed, whereupon the inspectors shall determine the
  amount for which and the hazards against which the bankrupt's property
  shall be insured by the trustee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Losses payable to trustee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} All insurance covering property of the bankrupt in
  force at the date of the bankruptcy shall in the event of loss
  suffered, without any notice to the insurer or other action on the
  part of the trustee and notwithstanding any statute or rule of law or
  contract or provision to a contrary effect, become payable immediately
  to the trustee as if the name of the trustee were written in the
  policy or contract of insurance as that of the insured or as if no
  change of title or ownership had come about and the trustee were the
  insured.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. B-3, s. 13
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trust account

- **[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  acting under the authority of this Act, a trustee shall, without
  delay, deposit in a bank all funds received for an estate in a
  separate trust account for each estate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other deposit-taking institutions must
  be insured

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The trustee may deposit the funds in a
  deposit-taking institution, other than a bank as defined in section 2,
  only if deposits held by that institution are insured or guaranteed
  under a provincial or federal enactment that provides depositors with
  protection against the loss of funds on deposit with that institution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign funds

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} If the funds are situated in a country other than
  Canada, the trustee may, if authorized by the Superintendent, deposit
  them in a financial institution in that country that is similar to a
  bank.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permission needed for certain acts

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} The trustee shall not withdraw any funds from the
  trust account of an estate without the permission in writing of the
  inspectors or, on application, the court, except for the payment of
  dividends and charges incidental to the administration of the estate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment by cheque

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} All payments made by a trustee under subsection (1.3)
  shall be made by cheque drawn on the estate account or in such manner
  as is specified in directives of the Superintendent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not in private account

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The trustee shall not deposit any funds received by
  the trustee when acting under the authority of this Act in any banking
  account kept by the trustee for the trustee's personal use.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 25
- 1992, c. 27, s. 10
- 1997, c. 12, s. 19
- 2005, c. 47, s. 20
- 2017, c. 26, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-25-20090918.html "Link to previous version of section 25"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Books to be kept by trustee

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  trustee shall keep proper books and records of the administration of
  each estate to which the trustee is appointed, in which shall be
  entered a record of all moneys received or disbursed by the trustee, a
  list of all creditors filing claims, the amount and disposition of
  those claims, a copy of all notices sent out, a signed copy of all
  minutes, proceedings had, and resolutions passed at any meeting of
  creditors or inspectors, court orders and all other matters or
  proceedings as may be necessary to give a complete account of the
  trustee's administration of the estate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee's records to be property of
  estate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The estate books, records and documents relating to
  the administration of an estate are deemed to be the property of the
  estate, and, in the event of any change of trustee, shall forthwith be
  delivered to the substituted trustee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records may be inspected

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The trustee shall permit the books, records and
  documents referred to in subsection (2) to be inspected and copies of
  them made by the Superintendent, the bankrupt or any creditor or their
  representative at any reasonable time.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 26
- 1997, c. 12, s. 20
- 2004, c. 25, s. 21
- [2019, c. 29, s. 161](#1156023-1170780){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156023-1170780.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26-20041215.html "Link to previous version of section 26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reports by trustee

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  trustee shall from time to time report,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} when required by the inspectors, to every creditor,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when required by any specific creditor, to the
    creditor, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} when required by the Superintendent, to the
    Superintendent or the creditors,

  showing the condition of the bankrupt's estate, the moneys on hand, if
  any, and particulars of any property remaining unsold.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disbursements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The trustee is entitled to charge against the estate
  of the bankrupt, for the preparation and delivery of any report
  referred to in subsection (1), only his actual disbursements.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. B-3, s. 13
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents to be forwarded to
Superintendent

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  trustee shall, without delay after their receipt or preparation, send
  to the Superintendent, in the prescribed manner, true copies of the
  documents referred to in section 155 and a true copy of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the notice referred to in section 102,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the statement referred to in paragraph 158(d),

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the trustee's final statement of receipts and
    disbursements and the dividend sheet, and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} every order made by the court on the application
    for discharge of a bankrupt or annulling any bankruptcy,

  and file a copy of the documents referred to in paragraphs (b) and (c)
  in the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notices, etc., to be forwarded to
  Superintendent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The trustee shall forward promptly to the
  Superintendent copies of all notices, reports and statements sent by
  him to the creditors and, when required, copies of such other
  documents as the Superintendent may specify.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 28
- 1992, c. 1, s. 12, c. 27, s. 11
- 2005, c. 47, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-28-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 28"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of trustee on expiration of licence
or removal

- **[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the licence of a trustee has been cancelled or
    suspended, or has ceased to be valid by reason of failure to pay
    fees,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a trustee has been removed from continuing the
    administration of an estate, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a trustee dies or becomes incapacitated,

  the trustee or the legal representative of the trustee shall, within
  such time as is fixed by the Superintendent, prepare and forward to
  the Superintendent a detailed financial statement of the receipts and
  disbursements together with a list of and report on the unadministered
  property of every estate under the trustee's administration for which
  the trustee has not been discharged, and shall forward to such other
  trustee as may be appointed in the trustee's stead or, pending the
  appointment of the other trustee, to the official receiver all the
  remaining property of every estate under the trustee's administration
  together with all the books, records and documents relating thereto.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2005, c. 47, s. 22\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 29
- 1997, c. 12, s. 21
- 2005, c. 47, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 29"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers exercisable by trustee with
permission of inspectors

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  trustee may, with the permission of the inspectors, do all or any of
  the following things:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sell or otherwise dispose of for such price or
    other consideration as the inspectors may approve all or any part of
    the property of the bankrupt, including the goodwill of the
    business, if any, and the book debts due or growing due to the
    bankrupt, by tender, public auction or private contract, with power
    to transfer the whole thereof to any person or company, or to sell
    the same in parcels;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} lease any real property or immovable;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} carry on the business of the bankrupt, in so far as
    may be necessary for the beneficial administration of the estate of
    the bankrupt;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} bring, institute or defend any action or other
    legal proceeding relating to the property of the bankrupt;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} employ a barrister or solicitor or, in the Province
    of Quebec, an advocate, or employ any other representative, to take
    any proceedings or do any business that may be sanctioned by the
    inspectors;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} accept as the consideration for the sale of any
    property of the bankrupt a sum of money payable at a future time,
    subject to such stipulations as to security and otherwise as the
    inspectors think fit;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} incur obligations, borrow money and give security
    on any property of the bankrupt by mortgage, hypothec, charge, lien,
    assignment, pledge or otherwise, such obligations and money borrowed
    to be discharged or repaid with interest out of the property of the
    bankrupt in priority to the claims of the creditors;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} compromise and settle any debts owing to the
    bankrupt;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} compromise any claim made by or against the estate;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} divide in its existing form among the creditors,
    according to its estimated value, any property that from its
    peculiar nature or other special circumstances cannot be readily or
    advantageously sold;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} elect to retain for the whole part of its unexpired
    term, or to assign, surrender, disclaim or resiliate any lease of,
    or other temporary interest or right in, any property of the
    bankrupt; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} appoint the bankrupt to aid in administering the
    estate of the bankrupt in such manner and on such terms as the
    inspectors may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permission limited to particular thing
  or class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The permission given for the purposes of
  subsection (1) is not a general permission to do all or any of the
  things mentioned in that subsection, but is only a permission to do
  the particular thing or things or class of thing or things that the
  permission specifies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If no inspectors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If no inspectors are appointed, the trustee may do
  all or any of the things referred to in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sale or disposal to related persons

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The trustee may sell or otherwise dispose of any of
  the bankrupt's property to a person who is related to the bankrupt
  only with the court's authorization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Related persons

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (4), in the case of a
  bankrupt other than an individual, a person who is related to the
  bankrupt includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a director or officer of the bankrupt;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who has or has had, directly or
    indirectly, control in fact of the bankrupt; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who is related to a person described in
    paragraph (a) or (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In deciding whether to grant the authorization, the
  court is to consider, among other things,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether the process leading to the proposed sale or
    disposition of the property was reasonable in the circumstances;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which the creditors were consulted;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the effects of the proposed sale or disposition on
    creditors and other interested parties;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether the consideration to be received for the
    property is reasonable and fair, taking into account the market
    value of the property;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether good faith efforts were made to sell or
    otherwise dispose of the property to persons who are not related to
    the bankrupt; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} whether the consideration to be received is
    superior to the consideration that would be received under any other
    offer made in accordance with the process leading to the proposed
    sale or disposition of the property.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 30
- 1997, c. 12, s. 22(F)
- 2004, c. 25, s. 22
- 2005, c. 47, s. 23
- 2007, c. 36, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-30-20041215.html "Link to previous version of section 30"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Borrowing powers with permission of court

- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} With
  the permission of the court, an interim receiver, a receiver within
  the meaning of subsection 243(2) or a trustee may make necessary or
  advisable advances, incur obligations, borrow money and give security
  on the debtor's property in any amount, on any terms and on any
  property that may be authorized by the court and those advances,
  obligations and money borrowed must be repaid out of the debtor's
  property in priority to the creditors' claims.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security under [Bank
  Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of giving security under section 427
  of the [Bank Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01), the interim receiver, receiver or
  trustee, when carrying on the business of the bankrupt, is deemed to
  be a person engaged in the class of business previously carried on by
  the bankrupt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limit of obligations and carrying on of
  business

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The creditors or inspectors may by resolution limit
  the amount of the obligations that may be incurred, the advances that
  may be made or moneys that may be borrowed by the trustee and may
  limit the period of time during which the business of the bankrupt may
  be carried on by the trustee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debts deemed to be debts of estate

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} All debts incurred and credit received in carrying on
  the business of a bankrupt are deemed to be debts incurred and credit
  received by the estate of the bankrupt.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 31
- 1991, c. 46, s. 584
- 2005, c. 47, s. 24
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-31-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 31"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee not obliged to carry on business

**[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** The trustee is not under
obligation to carry on the business of the bankrupt where in his opinion
the realizable value of the property of the bankrupt is insufficient to
protect him fully against possible loss occasioned by so doing and the
creditors or inspectors, on demand made by the trustee, neglect or
refuse to secure him against such possible loss.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. B-3, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reimbursement only of trustee's
disbursement advances

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** The court may make an
order providing for the sale of any or all of the assets of the estate
of the bankrupt, either by tender, private sale or public auction,
setting out the terms and conditions of the sale and directing that the
proceeds from the sale are to be used for the purpose of reimbursing the
trustee in respect of any costs that may be owing to the trustee or of
any moneys the trustee may have advanced as disbursements for the
benefit of the estate.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 33
- 2005, c. 47, s. 25
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-33-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 33"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee may apply to court for directions

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  trustee may apply to the court for directions in relation to any
  matter affecting the administration of the estate of a bankrupt and
  the court shall give in writing such directions, if any, as to it
  appear proper in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}To report to court after three years

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an estate has not been fully administered
  within three years after the bankruptcy, the trustee shall, if
  requested to do so by the Superintendent, report that fact to the
  court as soon as practicable thereafter, and the court shall make such
  order as it considers fit to expedite the administration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Superintendent's division
  office

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The trustee must send notice to the Superintendent's
  division office of the day and time when any application for
  directions made under subsection (1) is to be heard and of the day and
  time when the trustee intends to report to the court as required by
  the Superintendent under subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 34
- 1992, c. 27, s. 12
- 2005, c. 47, s. 26
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-34-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Redirection of mail

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  trustee may, by sending to the Canada Post Corporation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a notice in the prescribed form, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the trustee's certificate of appointment,

  request that any mail addressed to a bankrupt that is directed to any
  place referred to in the notice be redirected or sent by the Canada
  Post Corporation to the trustee or to such other person as the trustee
  may designate and when Canada Post Corporation receives those
  documents, it shall so redirect or send that mail.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permission for residence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice referred to in subsection (1) may refer to a
  bankrupt's residence only where the trustee has, on application,
  obtained permission from the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a bankrupt is an individual, a notice referred to
  in subsection (1) is operative only during the three-month period
  immediately after the date of the bankruptcy unless the court, on
  application, extends that period on any terms that it considers fit.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 35
- 1992, c. 27, s. 13
- 1997, c. 12, s. 23
- 2005, c. 47, s. 27
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-35-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 35"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of former trustee on substitution

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On the
  appointment of a substituted trustee, the former trustee shall without
  delay pass his or her accounts before the court and deliver to the
  substituted trustee all the property of the estate, together with all
  books, records and documents of the bankrupt and of the administration
  of the estate, as well as a statement of receipts and disbursements
  that contains a complete account of all moneys received by the trustee
  out of the property of the bankrupt or otherwise, the amount of
  interest received by the trustee, all moneys disbursed and expenses
  incurred and the remuneration claimed by the trustee, together with
  full particulars, description and value of all the bankrupt's property
  that has not been sold or realized, setting out the reason why the
  property has not been sold or realized and the disposition made of the
  property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of substituted trustee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A substituted trustee shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1992, c. 27, s. 14\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if appointed by the creditors, file with the court
    a copy of the minutes of the meeting signed by the chair;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} notify the Superintendent of his appointment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if required by the inspectors, register a notice of
    the appointment in the land register of any land titles or registry
    office where the assignment or bankruptcy order has been registered;
    and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} as soon as funds are available, pay to the former
    trustee his remuneration and disbursements as approved by the court.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 36
- 1992, c. 27, s. 14
- 1997, c. 12, s. 24
- 2004, c. 25, s. 23
- 2005, c. 47, ss. 28, 123(E)
- 2007, c. 36, s. 11(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-36-20041215.html "Link to previous version of section 36"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to court against trustee

**[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** Where the bankrupt or any
of the creditors or any other person is aggrieved by any act or decision
of the trustee, he may apply to the court and the court may confirm,
reverse or modify the act or decision complained of and make such order
in the premises as it thinks just.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. B-3, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceeding by creditor when trustee
refuses to act

- **[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a creditor requests the trustee to take any proceeding that in his
  opinion would be for the benefit of the estate of a bankrupt and the
  trustee refuses or neglects to take the proceeding, the creditor may
  obtain from the court an order authorizing him to take the proceeding
  in his own name and at his own expense and risk, on notice being given
  the other creditors of the contemplated proceeding, and on such other
  terms and conditions as the court may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer to creditor

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On an order under subsection (1) being made, the
  trustee shall assign and transfer to the creditor all his right, title
  and interest in the chose in action or subject-matter of the
  proceeding, including any document in support thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Benefits belong to creditor

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any benefit derived from a proceeding taken pursuant
  to subsection (1), to the extent of his claim and the costs, belongs
  exclusively to the creditor instituting the proceeding, and the
  surplus, if any, belongs to the estate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee may institute proceeding

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where, before an order is made under subsection (1),
  the trustee, with the permission of the inspectors, signifies to the
  court his readiness to institute the proceeding for the benefit of the
  creditors, the order shall fix the time within which he shall do so,
  and in that case the benefit derived from the proceeding, if
  instituted within the time so fixed, belongs to the estate.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 38
- 2004, c. 25, s. 24(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-38-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 38"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Remuneration of Trustee]{.HTitleText3} {#h-25076 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}To be voted by creditors

- **[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  remuneration of the trustee shall be such as is voted to the trustee
  by ordinary resolution at any meeting of creditors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not to exceed 7½ per cent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the remuneration of the trustee has not been
  fixed under subsection (1), the trustee may insert in his final
  statement and retain as his remuneration, subject to increase or
  reduction as hereinafter provided, a sum not exceeding seven and
  one-half per cent of the amount remaining out of the realization of
  the property of the debtor after the claims of the secured creditors
  have been paid or satisfied.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For carrying on debtor's business or in
  case of a proposal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the business of the debtor has been carried on
  by the trustee or under his supervision, he may be allowed such
  special remuneration for such services as the creditors or the
  inspectors may by resolution authorize, and, in the case of a
  proposal, such special remuneration as may be agreed to by the debtor,
  or in the absence of agreement with the debtor such amount as may be
  approved by the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Successive trustees

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the case of two or more trustees acting in
  succession, the remuneration shall be apportioned between the trustees
  in accordance with the services rendered by each, and in the absence
  of agreement between the trustees the court shall determine the amount
  payable to each.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court may increase or reduce

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On application by the trustee, a creditor or the
  debtor and on notice to such parties as the court may direct, the
  court may make an order increasing or reducing the remuneration.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. B-3, s. 21
:::

#### [Discharge of Trustee]{.HTitleText3} {#h-25090 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal of unrealizable property

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  property of a bankrupt that is listed in the statement of affairs
  referred to in paragraph 158(d) or otherwise disclosed to the trustee
  before the bankrupt's discharge and that is found incapable of
  realization must be returned to the bankrupt before the trustee's
  application for discharge, but if inspectors have been appointed, the
  trustee may do so only with their permission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final disposition of property

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a trustee is unable to dispose of any property
  as provided in this section, the court may make such order as it may
  consider necessary.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 40
- 2005, c. 47, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-40-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 40"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When a
  trustee has completed the duties required of him with respect to the
  administration of the property of a bankrupt, he shall apply to the
  court for a discharge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discharge of trustee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The court may discharge a trustee with respect to any
  estate on full administration thereof or, for sufficient cause, before
  full administration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When another trustee has been appointed

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A trustee when replaced by another trustee is
  entitled to be discharged if he has accounted to the satisfaction of
  the inspectors and the court for all property that came to his hands,
  and a period of three months has elapsed after the date of the
  replacement without any undisposed of claim or objection having been
  made by the bankrupt or any creditor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When estate deemed fully administered

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} When a trustee's accounts have been approved by the
  inspectors and taxed by the court and all objections, applications,
  oppositions, motions and appeals have been settled or disposed of and
  all dividends have been paid, the estate is deemed to have been fully
  administered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objections to be filed with court and
  trustee

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any interested person desiring to object to the
  discharge of a trustee shall, at least five days prior to the date of
  the hearing, file notice of objection with the registrar of the court
  setting out the reasons for the objection and serve a copy of the
  notice on the trustee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court may grant discharge

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The court shall consider the objection filed under
  subsection (5) and may grant or withhold a discharge accordingly or
  give such directions as it may deem proper in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fraud or breach of trust

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in or done under authority of this section
  relieves or discharges or shall be deemed to relieve or discharge a
  trustee from the results of any fraud.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of discharge of trustee

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The discharge of a trustee discharges him from all
  liability

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of any act done or default made by him
    in the administration of the property of the bankrupt, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in relation to his conduct as trustee,

  but any discharge may be revoked by the court on proof that it was
  obtained by fraud or by suppression or concealment of any material
  fact.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigation not precluded

  [(8.1)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (8) is to be construed as
  preventing an inquiry, investigation or proceeding in respect of a
  trustee under subsection 14.01(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security released

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The discharge of a trustee under this section
  operates as a release of the security provided pursuant to subsection
  16(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee remains

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding his discharge, the trustee remains
  the trustee of the estate for the performance of such duties as may be
  incidental to the full administration of the estate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of trustee by court to
  complete administration

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The court, on being satisfied that there are assets
  that have not been realized or distributed, may, on the application of
  any interested person, appoint a trustee to complete the
  administration of the estate of the bankrupt, and the trustee shall be
  governed by the provisions of this Act, in so far as they are
  applicable.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 41
- 1997, c. 12, s. 25
- 2004, c. 25, s. 25
- 2007, c. 36, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-41-20041215.html "Link to previous version of section 41"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[Bankruptcy Orders and Assignments]{.HTitleText1} {#h-25128 .Part}

### [Acts of Bankruptcy]{.HTitleText2} {#h-25129 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acts of bankruptcy

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  debtor commits an act of bankruptcy in each of the following cases:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if in Canada or elsewhere he makes an assignment of
    his property to a trustee for the benefit of his creditors
    generally, whether it is an assignment authorized by this Act or
    not;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if in Canada or elsewhere the debtor makes a
    fraudulent gift, delivery or transfer of the debtor's property or of
    any part of it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if in Canada or elsewhere the debtor makes any
    transfer of the debtor's property or any part of it, or creates any
    charge on it, that would under this Act be void or, in the Province
    of Quebec, null as a fraudulent preference;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if, with intent to defeat or delay his creditors,
    he departs out of Canada, or, being out of Canada, remains out of
    Canada, or departs from his dwelling-house or otherwise absents
    himself;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the debtor permits any execution or other
    process issued against the debtor under which any of the debtor's
    property is seized, levied on or taken in execution to remain
    unsatisfied until within five days after the time fixed by the
    executing officer for the sale of the property or for fifteen days
    after the seizure, levy or taking in execution, or if any of the
    debtor's property has been sold by the executing officer, or if the
    execution or other process has been held by the executing officer
    for a period of fifteen days after written demand for payment
    without seizure, levy or taking in execution or satisfaction by
    payment, or if it is returned endorsed to the effect that the
    executing officer can find no property on which to levy or to seize
    or take, but if interpleader or opposition proceedings have been
    instituted with respect to the property seized, the time elapsing
    between the date at which the proceedings were instituted and the
    date at which the proceedings are finally disposed of, settled or
    abandoned shall not be taken into account in calculating the period
    of fifteen days;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if he exhibits to any meeting of his creditors any
    statement of his assets and liabilities that shows that he is
    insolvent, or presents or causes to be presented to any such meeting
    a written admission of his inability to pay his debts;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} if he assigns, removes, secretes or disposes of or
    attempts or is about to assign, remove, secrete or dispose of any of
    his property with intent to defraud, defeat or delay his creditors
    or any of them;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} if he gives notice to any of his creditors that he
    has suspended or that he is about to suspend payment of his debts;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if he defaults in any proposal made under this Act;
    and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} if he ceases to meet his liabilities generally as
    they become due.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unauthorized assignments are void or
  null

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every assignment of an insolvent debtor's property
  other than an assignment authorized by this Act, made by an insolvent
  debtor for the general benefit of their creditors, is void or, in the
  Province of Quebec, null.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 42
- 1997, c. 12, s. 26
- 2004, c. 25, s. 27
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-42-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 42"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application for Bankruptcy Order]{.HTitleText2} {#h-25147 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bankruptcy application

- **[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section, one or more
  creditors may file in court an application for a bankruptcy order
  against a debtor if it is alleged in the application that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the debt or debts owing to the applicant creditor
    or creditors amount to one thousand dollars; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the debtor has committed an act of bankruptcy
    within the six months preceding the filing of the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If applicant creditor is a secured
  creditor

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the applicant creditor referred to in
  subsection (1) is a secured creditor, they shall in their application
  either state that they are willing to give up their security for the
  benefit of the creditors, in the event of a bankruptcy order being
  made against the debtor, or give an estimate of the value of the
  applicant creditor's security, and in the latter case they may be
  admitted as an applicant creditor to the extent of the balance of the
  debt due to them after deducting the value so estimated, in the same
  manner as if they were an unsecured creditor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affidavit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The application shall be verified by affidavit of the
  applicant or by someone duly authorized on their behalf having
  personal knowledge of the facts alleged in the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consolidation of applications

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If two or more applications are filed against the
  same debtor or against joint debtors, the court may consolidate the
  proceedings or any of them on any terms that the court thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Place of filing

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The application shall be filed in the court having
  jurisdiction in the judicial district of the locality of the debtor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of facts, etc.

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} At the hearing of the application, the court shall
  require proof of the facts alleged in the application and of the
  service of the application, and, if satisfied with the proof, may make
  a bankruptcy order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismissal of application

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the court is not satisfied with the proof of the
  facts alleged in the application or of the service of the application,
  or is satisfied by the debtor that the debtor is able to pay their
  debts, or that for other sufficient cause no order ought to be made,
  it shall dismiss the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismissal with respect to some
  respondents only

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If there are more respondents than one to an
  application, the court may dismiss the application with respect to one
  or more of them, without prejudice to the effect of the application as
  against the other or others of them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of trustee

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} On a bankruptcy order being made, the court shall
  appoint a licensed trustee as trustee of the property of the bankrupt,
  having regard, as far as the court considers just, to the wishes of
  the creditors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of proceedings if facts denied

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} If the debtor appears at the hearing of the
  application and denies the truth of the facts alleged in the
  application, the court may, instead of dismissing the application,
  stay all proceedings on the application on any terms that it may see
  fit to impose on the applicant as to costs or on the debtor to prevent
  alienation of the debtor's property and for any period of time that
  may be required for trial of the issue relating to the disputed facts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of proceedings for other reasons

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The court may for other sufficient reason make an
  order staying the proceedings under an application, either altogether
  or for a limited time, on any terms and subject to any conditions that
  the court may think just.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security for costs

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} Applicants who are resident out of Canada may be
  ordered to give security for costs to the debtor, and proceedings
  under the application may be stayed until the security is furnished.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bankruptcy order on another application

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} If proceedings on an application have been stayed or
  have not been prosecuted with due diligence and effect, the court may,
  if by reason of the delay or for any other cause it is considered
  just, substitute or add as applicant any other creditor to whom the
  debtor may be indebted in the amount required by this Act and make a
  bankruptcy order on the application of the other creditor, and shall,
  immediately after making the order, dismiss on any terms that it may
  consider just the application in the stayed or non-prosecuted
  proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawing application

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} An application shall not be withdrawn without the
  leave of the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application against one partner

  [(15)]{.lawlabel} Any creditor whose claim against a partnership is
  sufficient to entitle the creditor to present a bankruptcy application
  may present an application against any one or more partners of the
  firm without including the others.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court may consolidate proceedings

  [(16)]{.lawlabel} If a bankruptcy order has been made against one
  member of a partnership, any other application against a member of the
  same partnership shall be filed in or transferred to the same court,
  and the court may give any directions for consolidating the
  proceedings under the applications that it thinks just.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuance of proceedings on death of
  debtor

  [(17)]{.lawlabel} If a debtor against whom an application has been
  filed dies, the proceedings shall, unless the court otherwise orders,
  be continued as if the debtor were alive.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 43
- 1992, c. 27, s. 15
- 2004, c. 25, s. 28
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-43-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 43"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application against estate or succession

- **[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 43, an
  application for a bankruptcy order may be filed against the estate or
  succession of a deceased debtor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal liability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After service of an application for a bankruptcy
  order on the executor or administrator of the estate of a deceased
  debtor, or liquidator of the succession of a deceased debtor, the
  person on whom the order was served shall not make payment of any
  moneys or transfer any property of the deceased debtor, except as
  required for payment of the proper funeral and testamentary expenses,
  until the application is disposed of; otherwise, in addition to any
  penalties to which the person may be subject, the person is personally
  liable for the payment or transfer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Act done in good faith

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section invalidates any payment or
  transfer of property made or any act or thing done, in good faith, by
  the executor, administrator of the estate or liquidator of the
  succession before the service of an application referred to in
  subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 44
- 2004, c. 25, s. 28
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-44-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 44"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of application

- **[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  bankruptcy order is made, the costs of the applicant shall be taxed
  and be payable out of the estate, unless the court otherwise orders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insufficient proceeds

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the proceeds of the estate are not sufficient for
  the payment of any costs incurred by the trustee, the court may order
  the costs to be paid by the applicant.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 45
- 1992, c. 1, s. 14
- 2004, c. 25, s. 28
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-45-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 45"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Interim Receiver]{.HTitleText2} {#h-25203 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of interim receiver

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  court may, if it is shown to be necessary for the protection of the
  estate of a debtor, at any time after the filing of an application for
  a bankruptcy order and before a bankruptcy order is made, appoint a
  licensed trustee as interim receiver of the property or any part of
  the property of the debtor and direct the interim receiver to take
  immediate possession of the property or any part of it on an
  undertaking being given by the applicant that the court may impose
  with respect to interference with the debtor's legal rights and with
  respect to damages in the event of the application being dismissed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of interim receiver

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The interim receiver appointed under subsection (1)
  may, under the direction of the court, take conservatory measures and
  summarily dispose of property that is perishable or likely to
  depreciate rapidly in value and exercise such control over the
  business of the debtor as the court deems advisable, but the interim
  receiver shall not unduly interfere with the debtor in the carrying on
  of his business except as may be necessary for conservatory purposes
  or to comply with the order of the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Place of filing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An application under subsection (1) is to be filed in
  a court having jurisdiction in the judicial district of the locality
  of the debtor.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 46
- 1997, c. 12, s. 27(F)
- 2004, c. 25, s. 29
- 2007, c. 36, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-46-20041215.html "Link to previous version of section 46"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of interim receiver

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  court is satisfied that a notice is about to be sent or was sent under
  subsection 244(1), it may, subject to subsection (3), appoint a
  trustee as interim receiver of all or any part of the debtor's
  property that is subject to the security to which the notice relates
  until the earliest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the taking of possession by a receiver, within the
    meaning of subsection 243(2), of the debtor's property over which
    the interim receiver was appointed,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the taking of possession by a trustee of the
    debtor's property over which the interim receiver was appointed, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the expiry of 30 days after the day on which the
    interim receiver was appointed or of any period specified by the
    court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions to interim receiver

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The court may direct an interim receiver appointed
  under subsection (1) to do any or all of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} take possession of all or part of the debtor's
    property mentioned in the appointment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exercise such control over that property, and over
    the debtor's business, as the court considers advisable;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} take conservatory measures; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} summarily dispose of property that is perishable or
    likely to depreciate rapidly in value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When appointment may be made

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An appointment of an interim receiver may be made
  under subsection (1) only if it is shown to the court to be necessary
  for the protection of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the debtor's estate; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the interests of the creditor who sent the notice
    under subsection 244(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Place of filing

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An application under subsection (1) is to be filed in
  a court having jurisdiction in the judicial district of the locality
  of the debtor.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 47
- 1992, c. 27, s. 16
- 2005, c. 47, s. 30
- 2007, c. 36, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-47-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 47"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of interim receiver

- **[[47.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  a notice of intention has been filed under section 50.4 or a proposal
  has been filed under subsection 62(1), the court may at any time after
  the filing, subject to subsection (3), appoint as interim receiver of
  all or any part of the debtor's property,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the trustee under the notice of intention or
    proposal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} another trustee; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the trustee under the notice of intention or
    proposal and another trustee jointly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of appointment

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The appointment expires on the earliest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the taking of possession by a receiver, within the
    meaning of subsection 243(2), of the debtor's property over which
    the interim receiver was appointed,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the taking of possession by a trustee of the
    debtor's property over which the interim receiver was appointed, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} court approval of the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions to interim receiver

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The court may direct an interim receiver appointed
  under subsection (1) to do any or all of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} carry out the duties set out in subsection 50(10)
    or 50.4(7), in substitution for the trustee referred to in that
    subsection or jointly with that trustee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take possession of all or part of the debtor's
    property mentioned in the order of the court;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} exercise such control over that property, and over
    the debtor's business, as the court considers advisable;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} take conservatory measures; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} summarily dispose of property that is perishable or
    likely to depreciate rapidly in value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When appointment may be made

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An appointment of an interim receiver may be made
  under subsection (1) only if it is shown to the court to be necessary
  for the protection of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the debtor's estate; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the interests of one or more creditors, or of the
    creditors generally.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Place of filing

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An application under subsection (1) is to be filed in
  a court having jurisdiction in the judicial district of the locality
  of the debtor.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 16
- 2005, c. 47, s. 31
- 2007, c. 36, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-47.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 47.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders respecting fees and expenses

- **[[47.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an appointment of an interim receiver is made under section 47 or
  47.1, the court may make any order respecting the payment of fees and
  disbursements of the interim receiver that it considers proper,
  including an order giving the interim receiver security, ranking ahead
  of any or all secured creditors, over any or all of the assets of the
  debtor in respect of the interim receiver's claim for fees or
  disbursements, but the court shall not make such an order unless it is
  satisfied that all secured creditors who would be materially affected
  by the order were given reasonable advance notification and an
  opportunity to make representations to the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of disbursements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In subsection (1), "disbursements" do not include
  payments made in operating a business of the debtor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accounts, discharge of interim
  receivers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} With respect to interim receivers appointed under
  section 46, 47 or 47.1,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the form and content of their accounts, including
    their final statement of receipts and disbursements,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the procedure for the preparation and taxation of
    those accounts, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the procedure for the discharge of the interim
    receiver

  shall be as prescribed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 16
- 2004, c. 25, s. 30
- 2005, c. 47, s. 32
- 2015, c. 3, s. 7(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-47.2-20090918.html "Link to previous version of section 47.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of sections 43 to 46

**[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** Sections 43 to 46 do not
apply to individuals whose principal occupation and means of livelihood
is fishing, farming or the tillage of the soil or to any individual who
works for wages, salary, commission or hire at a rate of compensation
not exceeding twenty-five hundred dollars per year and does not on their
own account carry on business.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 48
- 1997, c. 12, s. 28
:::

### [Assignments]{.HTitleText2} {#h-25276 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment for general benefit of
creditors

- **[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  insolvent person or, if deceased, the executor or administrator of
  their estate or the liquidator of the succession, with the leave of
  the court, may make an assignment of all the insolvent person's
  property for the general benefit of the insolvent person's creditors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sworn statement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The assignment must be accompanied by a sworn
  statement in the prescribed form showing the debtor's property that is
  divisible among his or her creditors, the names and addresses of all
  his or her creditors and the amounts of their respective claims.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of assignment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The assignment made under subsection (1) shall be
  offered to the official receiver in the locality of the debtor, and it
  is inoperative until filed with that official receiver, who shall
  refuse to file the assignment unless it is in the prescribed form or
  to the like effect and accompanied by the sworn statement required by
  subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of trustee

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the official receiver files the assignment made
  under subsection (1), he shall appoint as trustee a licensed trustee
  whom he shall, as far as possible, select by reference to the wishes
  of the most interested creditors if ascertainable at the time, and the
  official receiver shall complete the assignment by inserting therein
  as grantee the name of the trustee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation of assignment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where the official receiver is unable to find a
  licensed trustee who is willing to act, the official receiver shall,
  after giving the bankrupt five days notice, cancel the assignment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure in small estates

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where the bankrupt is not a corporation and in the
  opinion of the official receiver the realizable assets of the
  bankrupt, after the claims of secured creditors are deducted, will not
  exceed five thousand dollars or such other amount as is prescribed,
  the provisions of this Act relating to the summary administration of
  estates shall apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Future property not to be considered

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In the determination of the realizable assets of a
  bankrupt for the purposes of subsection (6), no regard shall be had to
  any property that may be acquired by the bankrupt or devolve on the
  bankrupt before the bankrupt's discharge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where subsection (6) ceases to apply

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The official receiver may direct that subsection (6)
  shall cease to apply in respect of the bankrupt where the official
  receiver determines that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the realizable assets of the bankrupt, after the
    claims of secured creditors are deducted, exceed five thousand
    dollars or the amount prescribed, as the case may be, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the costs of realization of the assets of the
    bankrupt are a significant proportion of the realizable value of the
    assets,

  and the official receiver considers that such a direction is
  appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 49
- 1992, c. 1, s. 15, c. 27, s. 17
- 1997, c. 12, s. 29
- 2004, c. 25, s. 31(E)
- 2005, c. 47, s. 33
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-49-20041215.html "Link to previous version of section 49"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART III]{.HLabel1}[Proposals]{.HTitleText1} {#h-25299 .Part}

### [DIVISION I]{.HLabel2}[General Scheme for Proposals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-25300 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Who may make a proposal

- **[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (1.1), a
  proposal may be made by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an insolvent person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a receiver, within the meaning of subsection
    243(2), but only in relation to an insolvent person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a liquidator of an insolvent person's property;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a bankrupt; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a trustee of the estate of a bankrupt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where proposal may not be made

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} A proposal may not be made under this Division with
  respect to a debtor in respect of whom a consumer proposal has been
  filed under Division II until the administrator under the consumer
  proposal has been discharged.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}To whom proposal made

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} A proposal must be made to the creditors generally,
  either as a mass or separated into classes as provided in the
  proposal, and may also be made to secured creditors in respect of any
  class or classes of secured claim, subject to subsection (1.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} Where a proposal is made to one or more secured
  creditors in respect of secured claims of a particular class, the
  proposal must be made to all secured creditors in respect of secured
  claims of that class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classes of secured claims

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} Secured claims may be included in the same class if
  the interests or rights of the creditors holding those claims are
  sufficiently similar to give them a commonality of interest, taking
  into account

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the debts giving rise to the claims;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the nature and rank of the security in respect of
    the claims;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the remedies available to the creditors in the
    absence of the proposal, and the extent to which the creditors would
    recover their claims by exercising those remedies;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the treatment of the claims under the proposal, and
    the extent to which the claims would be paid under the proposal; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} such further criteria, consistent with those set
    out in paragraphs (a) to (d), as are prescribed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court may determine classes

  [(1.5)]{.lawlabel} The court may, on application made at any time
  after a notice of intention or a proposal is filed, determine, in
  accordance with subsection (1.4), the classes of secured claims
  appropriate to a proposal, and the class into which any particular
  secured claim falls.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Creditors' response

  [(1.6)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 50.1 as regards included secured
  creditors, any creditor may respond to the proposal as made to the
  creditors generally, by filing with the trustee a proof of claim in
  the manner provided for in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sections 124 to 126, in the case of unsecured
    creditors; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sections 124 to 134, in the case of secured
    creditors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of filing proof of claim

  [(1.7)]{.lawlabel} Hereinafter in this Division, a reference to an
  unsecured creditor shall be deemed to include a secured creditor who
  has filed a proof of claim under subsection (1.6), and a reference to
  an unsecured claim shall be deemed to include that secured creditor's
  claim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting

  [(1.8)]{.lawlabel} All questions relating to a proposal, except the
  question of accepting or refusing the proposal, shall be decided by
  ordinary resolution of the creditors to whom the proposal was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents to be filed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 50.4, proceedings for a proposal
  shall be commenced, in the case of an insolvent person, by filing with
  a licensed trustee, and in the case of a bankrupt, by filing with the
  trustee of the estate,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the proposal in writing setting out the
    terms of the proposal and the particulars of any securities or
    sureties proposed, signed by the person making the proposal and the
    proposed sureties if any; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the prescribed statement of affairs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of documents with the official
  receiver

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Copies of the documents referred to in
  subsection (2) must, at the time the proposal is filed under
  subsection 62(1), also be filed by the trustee with the official
  receiver in the locality of the debtor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of inspectors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A proposal made in respect of a bankrupt shall be
  approved by the inspectors before any further action is taken thereon.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposal, etc., not to be withdrawn

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No proposal or any security, guarantee or suretyship
  tendered with the proposal may be withdrawn pending the decision of
  the creditors and the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment not prevented

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) shall not be construed as preventing
  an insolvent person in respect of whom a proposal has been made from
  subsequently making an assignment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of trustee

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The trustee shall make or cause to be made such an
  appraisal and investigation of the affairs and property of the debtor
  as to enable the trustee to estimate with reasonable accuracy the
  financial situation of the debtor and the cause of the debtor's
  financial difficulties or insolvency and report the result thereof to
  the meeting of the creditors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee to file cash-flow statement

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The trustee shall, when filing a proposal under
  subsection 62(1) in respect of an insolvent person, file with the
  proposal

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a statement --- or a revised cash-flow statement if
    a cash-flow statement had previously been filed under subsection
    50.4(2) in respect of that insolvent person --- (in this section
    referred to as a "cash-flow statement") indicating the projected
    cash-flow of the insolvent person on at least a monthly basis,
    prepared by the person making the proposal, reviewed for its
    reasonableness by the trustee and signed by the trustee and the
    person making the proposal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a report on the reasonableness of the cash-flow
    statement, in the prescribed form, prepared and signed by the
    trustee; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a report containing prescribed representations by
    the person making the proposal regarding the preparation of the
    cash-flow statement, in the prescribed form, prepared and signed by
    the person making the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Creditors may obtain statement

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (8), any creditor may obtain a
  copy of the cash-flow statement on request made to the trustee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The court may order that a cash-flow statement or any
  part thereof not be released to some or all of the creditors pursuant
  to subsection (7) where it is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} such release would unduly prejudice the insolvent
    person; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} non-release would not unduly prejudice the creditor
    or creditors in question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee protected

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If the trustee acts in good faith and takes
  reasonable care in reviewing the cash-flow statement, he is not liable
  for loss or damage to any person resulting from that person's reliance
  on the cash-flow statement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee to monitor and report

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any direction of the court under
  paragraph 47.1(2)(a), the trustee under a proposal in respect of an
  insolvent person shall, for the purpose of monitoring the insolvent
  person's business and financial affairs, have access to and examine
  the insolvent person's property, including his premises, books,
  records and other financial documents, to the extent necessary to
  adequately assess the insolvent person's business and financial
  affairs, from the filing of the proposal until the proposal is
  approved by the court or the insolvent person becomes bankrupt, and
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} file a report on the state of the insolvent
    person's business and financial affairs --- containing the
    prescribed information, if any ---

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} with the official receiver without delay after
      ascertaining a material adverse change in the insolvent person's
      projected cash-flow or financial circumstances, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} with the court at any time that the court may
      order;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} send a report about the material adverse change
    to the creditors without delay after ascertaining the change; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} send, in the prescribed manner, a report on the
    state of the insolvent person's business and financial affairs ---
    containing the trustee's opinion as to the reasonableness of a
    decision, if any, to include in a proposal a provision that sections
    95 to 101 do not apply in respect of the proposal and containing the
    prescribed information, if any --- to the creditors and the official
    receiver at least 10 days before the day on which the meeting of
    creditors referred to in subsection 51(1) is to be held.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to creditors

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} An interim receiver who has been directed under
  subsection 47.1(2) to carry out the duties set out in subsection (10)
  in substitution for the trustee shall deliver a report on the state of
  the insolvent person's business and financial affairs, containing any
  prescribed information, to the trustee at least fifteen days before
  the meeting of creditors referred to in subsection 51(1), and the
  trustee shall send the report to the creditors and the official
  receiver, in the prescribed manner, at least ten days before the
  meeting of creditors referred to in that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court may declare proposal as deemed
  refused by creditors

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} The court may, on application by the trustee, the
  interim receiver, if any, appointed under section 47.1 or a creditor,
  at any time before the meeting of creditors, declare that the proposal
  is deemed to have been refused by the creditors if the court is
  satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the debtor has not acted, or is not acting, in good
    faith and with due diligence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the proposal will not likely be accepted by the
    creditors; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the creditors as a whole would be materially
    prejudiced if the application under this subsection is rejected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of declaration

  [(12.1)]{.lawlabel} If the court declares that the proposal is deemed
  to have been refused by the creditors, paragraphs 57(a) to (c) apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Claims against directors --- compromise

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} A proposal made in respect of a corporation may
  include in its terms provision for the compromise of claims against
  directors of the corporation that arose before the commencement of
  proceedings under this Act and that relate to the obligations of the
  corporation where the directors are by law liable in their capacity as
  directors for the payment of such obligations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} A provision for the compromise of claims against
  directors may not include claims that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} relate to contractual rights of one or more
    creditors arising from contracts with one or more directors; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are based on allegations of misrepresentation made
    by directors to creditors or of wrongful or oppressive conduct by
    directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

  [(15)]{.lawlabel} The court may declare that a claim against directors
  shall not be compromised if it is satisfied that the compromise would
  not be just and equitable in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of other provisions

  [(16)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 62(2) and section 122 apply, with such
  modifications as the circumstances require, in respect of claims
  against directors compromised under a proposal of a debtor
  corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of classes of claims

  [(17)]{.lawlabel} The court, on application made at any time after a
  proposal is filed, may determine the classes of claims of claimants
  against directors and the class into which any particular claimant's
  claim falls.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resignation or removal of directors

  [(18)]{.lawlabel} Where all of the directors have resigned or have
  been removed by the shareholders without replacement, any person who
  manages or supervises the management of the business and affairs of
  the corporation shall be deemed to be a director for the purposes of
  this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 50
- 1992, c. 27, s. 18
- 1997, c. 12, s. 30
- 2001, c. 4, s. 27(E)
- 2004, c. 25, s. 32
- 2005, c. 47, s. 34
- 2007, c. 36, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-50-20041215.html "Link to previous version of section 50"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Secured creditor may file proof of
secured claim

- **[[50.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (4), a
  secured creditor to whom a proposal has been made in respect of a
  particular secured claim may respond to the proposal by filing with
  the trustee a proof of secured claim in the prescribed form, and may
  vote, on all questions relating to the proposal, in respect of that
  entire claim, and sections 124 to 126 apply, in so far as they are
  applicable, with such modifications as the circumstances require, to
  proofs of secured claim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposed assessed value

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a proposal made to a secured creditor in
  respect of a claim includes a proposed assessed value of the security
  in respect of the claim, the secured creditor may file with the
  trustee a proof of secured claim in the prescribed form, and may vote
  as a secured creditor on all questions relating to the proposal in
  respect of an amount equal to the lesser of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the claim, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the proposed assessed value of the security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the proposed assessed value is less than the
  amount of the secured creditor's claim, the secured creditor may file
  with the trustee a proof of claim in the prescribed form, and may vote
  as an unsecured creditor on all questions relating to the proposal in
  respect of an amount equal to the difference between the amount of the
  claim and the proposed assessed value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a secured creditor is dissatisfied with the
  proposed assessed value of his security, the secured creditor may
  apply to the court, within fifteen days after the proposal is sent to
  the creditors, to have the proposed assessed value revised, and the
  court may revise the proposed assessed value, in which case the
  revised value henceforth applies for the purposes of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where no secured creditor in a class
  takes action

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where no secured creditor having a secured claim of a
  particular class files a proof of secured claim at or before the
  meeting of creditors, the secured creditors having claims of that
  class shall be deemed to have voted for the refusal of the proposal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 19
- 1997, c. 12, s. 31(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded secured creditor

**[[50.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50.2 .sectionLabel}** A secured creditor to
whom a proposal has not been made in respect of a particular secured
claim may not file a proof of secured claim in respect of that claim.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights in bankruptcy
