[Previous
Version](section-66-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION II]{.HLabel2}[Consumer Proposals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-25926 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[66.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.11 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,

[[administrator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[administrator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a trustee, or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person appointed or designated by the
      Superintendent to administer consumer
      proposals; ([administrateur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[consumer debtor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[consumer debtor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an individual who is
    bankrupt or insolvent and whose aggregate debts, excluding any debts
    secured by the individual's principal residence, are not more than
    \$250,000 or any other prescribed amount; ([débiteur
    consommateur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[consumer proposal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[consumer proposal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a proposal made
    under this Division. ([proposition de consommateur]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 45
- 2005, c. 47, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.11-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consumer proposal

- **[[66.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A consumer proposal may be made by a
  consumer debtor, subject to subsections (2) and 66.32(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dealing with certain consumer proposals
  together

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Two or more consumer proposals may, in such
  circumstances as are specified in directives of the Superintendent, be
  dealt with as one consumer proposal where they could reasonably be
  dealt with together because of the financial relationship of the
  consumer debtors involved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A consumer debtor who has filed a notice of intention
  or a proposal under Division I may not make a consumer proposal until
  the trustee appointed in respect of the notice of intention or
  proposal under Division I has been discharged.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}To whom consumer proposal is made

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A consumer proposal shall be made to the creditors
  generally.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Creditors' response

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any creditor may respond to a consumer proposal by
  filing with the administrator a proof of claim in the manner provided
  for in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sections 124 to 126, in the case of unsecured
    creditors; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sections 124 to 134, in the case of secured
    creditors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of consumer proposal

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A consumer proposal must provide that its performance
  is to be completed within five years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Priority of claims, fees

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A consumer proposal must provide

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the payment in priority to other claims of all
    claims directed to be so paid in the distribution of the property of
    the consumer debtor;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the payment of all prescribed fees and expenses

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} of the administrator on and incidental to
      proceedings arising out of the consumer proposal, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} of any person in respect of counselling provided
      pursuant to paragraph 66.13(2)(b); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for the manner of distributing dividends.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 46
- 2005, c. 47, s. 47(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.12-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commencement of proceedings

- **[[66.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A consumer debtor who wishes to make
  a consumer proposal shall commence proceedings by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtaining the assistance of an administrator in
    preparing the consumer proposal; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} providing the administrator with the prescribed
    information on the consumer debtor's current financial situation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of administrator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An administrator who agrees to assist a consumer
  debtor shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} investigate, or cause to be investigated, the
    consumer debtor's property and financial affairs so as to be able to
    assess with reasonable accuracy the consumer debtor's financial
    situation and the cause of his insolvency;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide, or provide for, counselling in accordance
    with directives issued by the Superintendent pursuant to paragraph
    5(4)(b);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prepare a consumer proposal in the prescribed form;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (3), file with the official
    receiver a copy of the consumer proposal, signed by the consumer
    debtor, and the prescribed statement of affairs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where consumer proposal not to be filed

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The administrator shall not file a consumer proposal
  under paragraph (2)(d) if he has reason to believe that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the debtor is not eligible to make a consumer
    proposal; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there has been non-compliance with anything
    required by this section or section 66.12.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where consumer proposal wrongly filed

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the administrator determines, after filing a
  consumer proposal under paragraph (2)(d), that it should not have been
  filed because the debtor was not eligible to make a consumer proposal,
  the administrator shall forthwith so inform the creditors and the
  official receiver, but the consumer proposal is not invalid by reason
  only that the debtor was not eligible to make the consumer proposal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1999, c. 31, s. 21(E)
- 2005, c. 47, s. 48
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.13-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.13"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of administrator

**[[66.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.14 .sectionLabel}** The administrator
shall, within ten days after filing a consumer proposal with the
official receiver,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prepare and file with the official receiver a report
  in the prescribed form setting out

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the results of the investigation made under
    paragraph 66.13(2)(a),

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the administrator's opinion as to whether the
    consumer proposal is reasonable and fair to the consumer debtor and
    the creditors, and whether the consumer debtor will be able to
    perform it, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2005, c. 47, s. 49\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a list of the creditors whose claims exceed two
    hundred and fifty dollars; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} send to every known creditor, in the prescribed form
  and manner,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the consumer proposal and a copy of the
    statement of affairs referred to in paragraph 66.13(2)(d),

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the report referred to in paragraph (a),

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a form of proof of claim as prescribed, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a statement explaining that a meeting of creditors
    will be called only if required under section 66.15 and that a
    review of the consumer proposal by a court will be made only if it
    is requested in accordance with subsection 66.22(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 47
- 2005, c. 47, s. 49
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.14-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.14"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meeting of creditors

- **[[66.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The official receiver may, at any
  time within the forty-five day period following the filing of the
  consumer proposal, direct the administrator to call a meeting of
  creditors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The administrator shall call a meeting of creditors

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} forthwith after being so directed by the official
    receiver under subsection (1), or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at the expiration of the forty-five day period
    following the filing of the consumer proposal, if at that time
    creditors having in the aggregate at least twenty-five per cent in
    value of the proven claims have so requested,

  and any meeting of creditors must be held within twenty-one days after
  being called.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to be sent to creditors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The administrator shall, at least ten days before a
  meeting called pursuant to this section, send to the consumer debtor,
  every known creditor and the official receiver, in the prescribed form
  and manner, a notice setting out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the time and place of the meeting;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a form of proxy as prescribed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} such other information and documentation as is
    prescribed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 48
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chair of meeting

- **[[66.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The official receiver, or the
  nominee thereof, shall be the chair of a meeting called pursuant to
  section 66.15 and subsection 66.37(1) and shall decide any questions
  or disputes arising at the meeting, and any creditor may appeal any
  such decision to the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment of meeting for further
  investigation and examination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the creditors by ordinary resolution at the
  meeting so require, the meeting shall be adjourned to such time and
  place as may be fixed by the chair

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to enable a further appraisal and investigation of
    the affairs and property of the consumer debtor to be made; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the examination under oath of the consumer
    debtor or of such other person as may be believed to have knowledge
    of the affairs or property of the consumer debtor, and the testimony
    of the consumer debtor or such other person, if transcribed, shall
    be placed before the adjourned meeting or may be read in court on
    the application, if any, for the approval of the consumer proposal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 2005, c. 47, s. 123(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.16-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.16"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Creditor may indicate assent or dissent

- **[[66.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any creditor who has proved a claim
  may indicate assent to or dissent from the consumer proposal in the
  prescribed manner to the administrator at or prior to a meeting of
  creditors, or prior to the expiration of the forty-five day period
  following the filing of the consumer proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of assent or dissent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless it is rescinded, any assent or dissent
  received by the administrator at or before a meeting of creditors has
  effect as if the creditor had been present and had voted at the
  meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 49
- 2005, c. 47, s. 50
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.17-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.17"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where consumer proposal deemed accepted

- **[[66.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where, at the expiration of the
  forty-five day period following the filing of the consumer proposal,
  no obligation has arisen under subsection 66.15(2) to call a meeting
  of creditors, the consumer proposal is deemed to be accepted by the
  creditors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where there is no quorum at a meeting of creditors,
  the consumer proposal shall be deemed to be accepted by the creditors.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 50
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting on consumer proposal

- **[[66.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At a meeting of creditors, the
  creditors may by ordinary resolution, voting all as one class, accept
  or refuse the consumer proposal as filed or as altered at the meeting
  or any adjournment thereof, subject to the rights of secured
  creditors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Related creditor

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A creditor who is related to the consumer debtor may
  vote against but not for the acceptance of the consumer proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting by administrator

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The administrator, as a creditor, may not vote on the
  consumer proposal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Creditors may provide for supervision of
consumer debtor's affairs

**[[66.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.2 .sectionLabel}** The creditors, with
the consent of the consumer debtor, may include such provisions or terms
in the consumer proposal with respect to the supervision of the affairs
of the consumer debtor as they may deem advisable.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of inspectors

**[[66.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.21 .sectionLabel}** The creditors may
appoint up to three inspectors of the estate of the consumer debtor, who
shall have the powers of an inspector under this Act, subject to any
extension or restriction of those powers by the terms of the consumer
proposal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court

- **[[66.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a consumer proposal is
  accepted or deemed accepted by the creditors, the administrator shall,
  if requested by the official receiver or any other interested party
  within fifteen days after the day of acceptance or deemed acceptance,
  forthwith apply to the court to have the consumer proposal reviewed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where consumer proposal deemed approved
  by court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, at the expiration of the fifteenth day after
  the day of acceptance or deemed acceptance of the consumer proposal by
  the creditors, no obligation has arisen under subsection (1) to apply
  to the court, the consumer proposal is deemed to be approved by the
  court.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 51
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure for application to court

**[[66.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.23 .sectionLabel}** Where the
administrator applies to the court pursuant to subsection 66.22(1), the
administrator shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} send a notice of the hearing of the application, in
  the prescribed manner and at least fifteen days before the date of the
  hearing, to the consumer debtor, to every creditor who has proved a
  claim and to the official receiver;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} forward a copy of the report referred to in
  paragraph (c) to the official receiver at least ten days before the
  date of the hearing; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} at least two days before the date of the hearing,
  file with the court a report in the prescribed form on the consumer
  proposal and the conduct of the consumer debtor.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 52
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court to hear report of administrator,
etc.

- **[[66.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.24
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The court shall, before approving
  the consumer proposal, hear the report mentioned in paragraph 66.23(c)
  and, in addition, shall hear the official receiver, the administrator,
  the consumer debtor, any opposing, objecting or dissenting creditor or
  other interested party, and such further evidence as the court may
  require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal to approve the consumer
  proposal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the court is of the opinion that the terms of
  the consumer proposal are not reasonable or are not fair to the
  consumer debtor and the creditors, the court shall refuse to approve
  the consumer proposal, and the court may refuse to approve the
  consumer proposal whenever it is established that the consumer debtor

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has committed any one of the offences mentioned in
    sections 198 to 200; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} was not eligible to make a consumer proposal when
    the consumer proposal was filed with the official receiver.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposal must comply with Act

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The court shall refuse to approve a consumer proposal
  if it does not comply with subsections 66.12(5) and (6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of court

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (1) to (3), the court may
  either approve or refuse to approve the consumer proposal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of consumer proposal

**[[66.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.25 .sectionLabel}** A consumer debtor
may withdraw a consumer proposal

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} at any time before its deemed approval by the court
  by virtue of subsection 66.22(2), where no court review is requested;
  or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where a court review is requested, at any time before
  its actual approval or refusal by the court pursuant to section 66.24.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where periodic payments not provided for

**[[66.251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.251 .sectionLabel}** Where a proposal
is approved or deemed approved by the court and the terms of the
proposal do not provide for the distribution of available moneys at
least once every three months, the administrator shall forthwith, upon
ascertaining any change in the consumer debtor's circumstances that
leads the administrator to conclude, after consultation with the debtor
where practicable, that such change could jeopardize the consumer
debtor's ability to meet the terms of the proposal, in writing, notify
the official receiver and every known creditor of the change.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 12, s. 53
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payments to administrator

- **[[66.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All moneys payable under the
  consumer proposal shall be paid to the administrator and, after
  payment of all fees and expenses mentioned in paragraph 66.12(6)(b),
  the administrator shall distribute available moneys to the creditors
  in accordance with the terms of the consumer proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deposit of moneys

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In such circumstances as are specified in directives
  of the Superintendent and with the approval of the Superintendent, the
  administrator may deposit all moneys relating to the administration of
  consumer proposals in a single trust account.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Section 147 applies

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Section 147 applies, with such modifications as the
  circumstances require, to all distributions made to the creditors by
  the administrator pursuant to subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 54
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notifications

**[[66.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.27 .sectionLabel}** The administrator
shall, within five days after

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the refusal of a consumer proposal by the creditors,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the refusal of a consumer proposal by the court, and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the withdrawal of a consumer proposal by the consumer
  debtor,

so notify in the prescribed form and manner the consumer debtor, every
known creditor and the official receiver.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 55
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for determining claims

- **[[66.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.28
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The time with respect to which the
  claims of creditors shall be determined is the time of the filing of
  the consumer proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}On whom approval binding

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2.1), a consumer proposal
  accepted, or deemed accepted, by the creditors and approved, or deemed
  approved, by the court is binding on creditors in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all unsecured claims; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} secured claims for which proofs of claim have been
    filed in the manner provided for in sections 124 to 134.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When consumer debtor is released from
  debt

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A consumer proposal accepted, or deemed accepted,
  by the creditors and approved, or deemed approved, by the court does
  not release the consumer debtor from any particular debt or liability
  referred to in subsection 178(1) unless the consumer proposal
  explicitly provides for the compromise of that debt or liability and
  the creditor in relation to that debt or liability voted for the
  acceptance of the consumer proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain persons not released

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The acceptance of a consumer proposal by a creditor
  does not release any person who would not be released under this Act
  by the discharge of the consumer debtor.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 2005, c. 47, s. 51
- 2007, c. 36, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.28-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.28"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrator may issue certificate

- **[[66.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.29
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a consumer proposal is approved
  or deemed approved by the court, the administrator may, if the
  administrator believes on reasonable grounds that the debtor owns land
  or other valuable property, issue a certificate in respect of the
  proposal, and may cause the certificate to be filed in any place where
  a certificate of judgment, writ of seizure and sale or other like
  document may be filed or where a legal hypothec of judgment creditors
  may be registered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of filing certificate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A certificate filed under subsection (1) operates as
  a certificate of judgment, writ of execution or legal hypothec of
  judgment creditors until the proposal is fully performed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 2004, c. 25, s. 40
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.29-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.29"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annulment of consumer proposal

- **[[66.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where default is made in the
  performance of any provision in a consumer proposal, or where it
  appears to the court

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the debtor was not eligible to make a consumer
    proposal when the consumer proposal was filed,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the consumer proposal cannot continue without
    injustice or undue delay, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that the approval of the court was obtained by
    fraud,

  the court may, on application, with such notice as the court may
  direct to the consumer debtor and, if applicable, to the administrator
  and to the creditors, annul the consumer proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of things done

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) shall be made
  without prejudice to the validity of any sale, disposition of property
  or payment duly made, or anything duly done under or in pursuance of
  the consumer proposal, and notwithstanding the annulment of the
  consumer proposal, a guarantee given pursuant to the consumer proposal
  remains in full force and effect in accordance with its terms.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annulment for offence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A consumer proposal, although accepted or approved,
  may be annulled by order of the court at the request of the
  administrator or of any creditor whenever the consumer debtor is
  afterwards convicted of any offence under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of annulment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where an order annulling the consumer proposal of a
  consumer debtor who is not a bankrupt has been made pursuant to this
  section, the administrator shall forthwith so inform the creditors and
  file a report thereof in the prescribed form with the official
  receiver.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annulment effect

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a consumer proposal made by a bankrupt is
  annulled,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the consumer debtor is deemed on the annulment to
    have made an assignment and the order annulling the proposal shall
    so state;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the trustee who is the administrator of the
    proposal shall, within five days after the order is made, send
    notice of the meeting of creditors under section 102, at which
    meeting the creditors may by ordinary resolution, notwithstanding
    section 14, affirm the appointment of the trustee or appoint another
    trustee in lieu of that trustee; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the trustee shall forthwith file a report thereof
    in the prescribed form with the official receiver, who shall
    thereupon issue a certificate of assignment in the prescribed form,
    which has the same effect for the purposes of this Act as an
    assignment filed pursuant to section 49.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 56
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed annulment --- default of payment

- **[[66.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the court has previously
  ordered otherwise or unless an amendment to the consumer proposal has
  previously been filed, a consumer proposal is deemed to be annulled on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case when payments under the consumer
    proposal are to be made monthly or more frequently, the day on which
    the consumer debtor is in default for an amount that is equal to or
    more than the amount of three payments; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case when payments under the consumer
    proposal are to be made less frequently than monthly, the day that
    is three months after the day on which the consumer debtor is in
    default in respect of any payment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed annulment --- amendment
  withdrawn or refused

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an amendment to a consumer proposal filed before
  the deemed annulment of the consumer proposal under subsection (1) is
  withdrawn or refused by the creditors or the court, the consumer
  proposal is deemed to be annulled at the time that the amendment is
  withdrawn or refused.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of administrator in relation to
  deemed annulment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Without delay after a consumer proposal is deemed to
  be annulled, the administrator shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} file with the official receiver a report in the
    prescribed form in relation to the deemed annulment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} send a notice to the creditors informing them of
    the deemed annulment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effects of deemed annulment ---
  consumer proposal made by a bankrupt

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a consumer proposal made by a bankrupt is deemed
  to be annulled,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the consumer debtor is deemed to have made an
    assignment on the day on which the consumer proposal is deemed to be
    annulled;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the trustee who is the administrator of the
    consumer proposal shall, within five days after the day on which the
    consumer proposal is deemed to be annulled, send notice of the
    meeting of creditors under section 102, at which meeting the
    creditors may by ordinary resolution, despite section 14, affirm the
    appointment of the trustee or appoint another trustee in lieu of
    that trustee; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the trustee shall, without delay, file with the
    official receiver, in the prescribed form, a report of the deemed
    annulment and the official receiver shall, without delay, issue a
    certificate of assignment, in the prescribed form, which has the
    same effect for the purposes of this Act as an assignment filed
    under section 49.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of things done before deemed
  annulment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A deemed annulment of a consumer proposal does not
  prejudice the validity of any sale or disposition of property or
  payment duly made, or anything duly done under or in pursuance of the
  consumer proposal and, despite the deemed annulment, a guarantee given
  under the consumer proposal remains in full force and effect in
  accordance with its terms.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of possibility of consumer
  proposal being automatically revived

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a deemed annulment of a consumer
  proposal made by a person other than a bankrupt, if the administrator
  considers it appropriate to do so in the circumstances, he or she may,
  with notice to the official receiver, send to the creditors --- within
  30 days, or any other number of days that is prescribed, after the day
  on which the consumer proposal was deemed to be annulled --- a notice
  in the prescribed form informing them that the consumer proposal will
  be automatically revived 60 days, or any other number of days that is
  prescribed, after the day on which it was deemed to be annulled unless
  one of them files with the administrator, in the prescribed manner, a
  notice of objection to the revival.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Automatic revival

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the notice is sent by the administrator and no
  notice of objection is filed during the period referred to in
  subsection (6), the consumer proposal is automatically revived on the
  expiry of that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice if no automatic revival

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If a notice of objection is filed during the period
  referred to in subsection (6), the administrator is to send, without
  delay, to the official receiver and to each creditor a notice in the
  prescribed form informing them that the consumer proposal is not going
  to be automatically revived on the expiry of that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrator may apply to court to
  revive consumer proposal

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The administrator may at any time apply to the court,
  with notice to the official receiver and the creditors, for an order
  reviving any consumer proposal of a consumer debtor who is not a
  bankrupt that was deemed to be annulled, and the court, if it
  considers it appropriate to do so in the circumstances, may make an
  order reviving the consumer proposal, on any terms that the court
  considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of administrator if consumer
  proposal is revived

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Without delay after a consumer proposal is revived,
  the administrator shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} file with the official receiver a report in the
    prescribed form in relation to the revival; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} send a notice to the creditors informing them of
    the revival.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of things done before revival

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The revival of a consumer proposal does not
  prejudice the validity of anything duly done --- between the day on
  which the consumer proposal is deemed to be annulled and the day on
  which it is revived --- by a creditor in the exercise of any rights
  revived by subsection 66.32(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 2005, c. 47, s. 52
- 2007, c. 36, s. 30
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.31-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.31"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effects of annulment

- **[[66.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.32
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the court otherwise orders,
  where a consumer proposal is annulled or deemed annulled, the consumer
  debtor

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} may not make another consumer proposal, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is not entitled to any relief provided by sections
    69 to 69.2

  until all claims for which proofs of claim were filed and accepted are
  either paid in full or are extinguished by the operation of subsection
  178(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a consumer proposal is annulled or deemed
  annulled, the rights of the creditors are revived for the amount of
  their claims less any dividends received.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 2005, c. 47, s. 53(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.32-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.32"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[66.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.33 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2005,
c. 47, s. 54\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.33-20041215.html "Link to previous version of section 66.33"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain rights limited

- **[[66.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.34
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a consumer proposal has been
  filed in respect of a consumer debtor, no person may terminate or
  amend any agreement, including a security agreement, with the consumer
  debtor, or claim an accelerated payment, or the forfeiture of the
  term, under any agreement, including a security agreement, with the
  consumer debtor, by reason only that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the consumer debtor is insolvent, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a consumer proposal has been filed in respect of
    the consumer debtor

  until the consumer proposal has been withdrawn, refused by the
  creditors or the court, annulled or deemed annulled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the agreement referred to in subsection (1) is
  a lease, subsection (1) shall be read as including the following
  paragraph:

  > ::: ReadAsText
  > - ["(c)]{.lawlabel} the consumer debtor has not paid rent in respect
  >   of a period preceding the filing of the consumer proposal."
  > :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a consumer proposal has been filed in respect
  of a consumer debtor, no public utility may discontinue service to
  that consumer debtor by reason only that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the consumer debtor is insolvent,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a consumer proposal has been filed in respect of
    the consumer debtor, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the consumer debtor has not paid for services
    rendered, or material provided, before the filing of the consumer
    proposal

  until the consumer proposal has been withdrawn, refused by the
  creditors or the court, annulled or deemed annulled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain acts not prevented

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsections (1) to (3) shall be construed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as prohibiting a person from requiring payments to
    be made in cash for goods, services, use of leased property or other
    valuable consideration provided after the filing of the consumer
    proposal; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as requiring the further advance of money or
    credit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provisions of section override
  agreement

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any provision in an agreement that has the effect of
  providing for, or permitting, anything that, in substance, is contrary
  to subsections (1) to (3) is of no force or effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The court may, on application by a party to an
  agreement or by a public utility, declare that this section does not
  apply, or applies only to the extent declared by the court, where the
  applicant satisfies the court that the operation of this section would
  likely cause it significant financial hardship.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligible financial contracts

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of an
  eligible financial contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permitted actions

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 69.2, the following actions are
  permitted in respect of an eligible financial contract that is entered
  into before the filing of a consumer proposal and is terminated on or
  after that filing, but only in accordance with the provisions of that
  contract:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the netting or setting off or compensation of
    obligations between the consumer debtor and the other parties to the
    eligible financial contract; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any dealing with financial collateral including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the sale or foreclosure or, in the Province of
      Quebec, the surrender of financial collateral, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the setting off or compensation of financial
      collateral or the application of the proceeds or value of
      financial collateral.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Net termination values

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If net termination values determined in accordance
  with an eligible financial contract referred to in subsection (8) are
  owed by the consumer debtor to another party to the eligible financial
  contract, that other party is deemed, for the purposes of subsection
  69.2(1), to be a creditor of the consumer debtor with a claim provable
  in bankruptcy in respect of those net termination values.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 2004, c. 25, s. 42(E)
- 2005, c. 47, s. 55
- 2007, c. 29, s. 94
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.34-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 66.34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment of wages

- **[[66.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.35
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An assignment of existing or future
  wages made by a consumer debtor before the filing of a consumer
  proposal is of no effect in respect of wages earned after the filing
  of the consumer proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment of debts at request of
  administrator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In order to ensure compliance with the terms of a
  consumer proposal, the administrator may, at any time after the
  consumer proposal is filed, require of, and take from, the consumer
  debtor an assignment of any amount payable to the consumer debtor,
  including wages, that may become payable in the future, but no such
  assignment can, unless the consumer debtor agrees, be for an amount
  greater than is due and payable pursuant to the terms of the consumer
  proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Third parties protected

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An assignment made pursuant to subsection (2) is of
  no effect against a person owing the amount payable until a notice of
  the assignment is served on that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When section ceases to apply

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section ceases to apply where the consumer
  proposal is refused by the creditors or by the court, or is withdrawn,
  annulled or deemed annulled.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 57
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No dismissal, etc., of employee

**[[66.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.36 .sectionLabel}** No employer shall
dismiss, suspend, lay off or otherwise discipline a consumer debtor on
the sole ground that a consumer proposal has been filed in respect of
that consumer debtor.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment to consumer proposal

**[[66.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.37 .sectionLabel}** If an administrator
files an amendment to a consumer proposal before the withdrawal,
refusal, approval or deemed approval by the court of the consumer
proposal, or after the approval or deemed approval by the court of the
consumer proposal and before it has been fully performed or annulled or
deemed annulled, the provisions of this Division apply to the consumer
proposal and the amended consumer proposal, with any modifications that
the circumstances require, and, for that purpose, the definition
[[consumer debtor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in section 66.11 is to be read
as follows:

> ::: ReadAsText
>
> [[consumer debtor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
>
> :   [[consumer debtor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an individual who is
>     insolvent;
> :::

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 2005, c. 47, s. 56
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.37-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.37"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate if consumer proposal
performed

- **[[66.38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.38
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a consumer proposal is fully
  performed, the administrator shall issue a certificate to that effect,
  in the prescribed form, to the consumer debtor and to the official
  receiver.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect if counselling refused

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a
  consumer debtor who has refused or neglected to receive counselling
  provided under paragraph 66.13(2)(b).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 2005, c. 47, s. 56
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66.38-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66.38"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrator's accounts, discharge

**[[66.39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.39 .sectionLabel}** The form and
content of the administrator's accounts, the procedure for the
preparation and taxation of those accounts and the procedure for the
discharge of the administrator shall be as prescribed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Act to apply

- **[[66.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All the provisions of this Act,
  except Division I of this Part, in so far as they are applicable,
  apply, with such modifications as the circumstances require, to
  consumer proposals.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where consumer debtor is bankrupt

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a consumer proposal is made by a consumer
  debtor who is a bankrupt,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the consumer proposal must be approved by the
    inspectors, if any, before any further action is taken thereon;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the consumer debtor must have obtained the
    assistance of a trustee who shall act as administrator of the
    proposal in the preparation and execution thereof;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the time with respect to which the claims of
    creditors shall be determined is the time at which the consumer
    debtor became bankrupt; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the approval or deemed approval by the court of the
    consumer proposal operates to annul the bankruptcy and to revest in
    the consumer debtor, or in such other person as the court may
    approve, all the right, title and interest of the trustee in the
    property of the consumer debtor, unless the terms of the consumer
    proposal otherwise provide.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 32
- 1997, c. 12, s. 58
:::

## [PART IV]{.HLabel1}[Property of the Bankrupt]{.HTitleText1} {#h-26268 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property of bankrupt

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  property of a bankrupt divisible among his creditors shall not
  comprise

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} property held by the bankrupt in trust for any
    other person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any property that as against the bankrupt is exempt
    from execution or seizure under any laws applicable in the province
    within which the property is situated and within which the bankrupt
    resides;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} goods and services tax credit payments that are
    made in prescribed circumstances to the bankrupt and that are not
    property referred to in paragraph (a) or (b);

  - [(b.2)]{.lawlabel} prescribed payments relating to the essential
    needs of an individual that are made in prescribed circumstances to
    the bankrupt and that are not property referred to in paragraph (a)
    or (b); or

  - [(b.3)]{.lawlabel} without restricting the generality of paragraph
    (b), property in a [[registered retirement savings
    plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, a [[registered retirement income
    fund]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or a [[registered disability savings
    plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as those expressions are defined in
    the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or in any prescribed plan,
    other than property contributed to any such plan or fund in the 12
    months before the date of bankruptcy,

  but it shall comprise

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all property wherever situated of the bankrupt at
    the date of the bankruptcy or that may be acquired by or devolve on
    the bankrupt before their discharge, including any refund owing to
    the bankrupt under the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) in respect
    of the calendar year --- or the fiscal year of the bankrupt if it is
    different from the calendar year --- in which the bankrupt became a
    bankrupt, except the portion that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is not subject to the operation of this Act, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a bankrupt who is the judgment
      debtor named in a garnishee summons served on Her Majesty under
      the [Family Orders and Agreements Enforcement Assistance
      Act](/eng/acts/F-1.4), is garnishable money that is payable to the
      bankrupt and is to be paid under the garnishee summons, and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} such powers in or over or in respect of the
    property as might have been exercised by the bankrupt for his own
    benefit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed trusts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), notwithstanding any
  provision in federal or provincial legislation that has the effect of
  deeming property to be held in trust for Her Majesty, property of a
  bankrupt shall not be regarded as held in trust for Her Majesty for
  the purpose of paragraph (1)(a) unless it would be so regarded in the
  absence of that statutory provision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply in respect of amounts
  deemed to be held in trust under subsection 227(4) or (4.1) of the
  [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), subsection 23(3) or (4) of the
  [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) or subsection 86(2) or (2.1) of
  the [Employment Insurance Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) (each of which is in
  this subsection referred to as a "federal provision") nor in respect
  of amounts deemed to be held in trust under any law of a province that
  creates a deemed trust the sole purpose of which is to ensure
  remittance to Her Majesty in right of the province of amounts deducted
  or withheld under a law of the province where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that law of the province imposes a tax similar in
    nature to the tax imposed under the [Income Tax
    Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) and the amounts deducted or withheld under
    that law of the province are of the same nature as the amounts
    referred to in subsection 227(4) or (4.1) of the [Income Tax
    Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the province is a [[province providing a
    comprehensive pension plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in
    subsection 3(1) of the [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8), that
    law of the province establishes a [[provincial pension
    plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in that subsection and the
    amounts deducted or withheld under that law of the province are of
    the same nature as amounts referred to in subsection 23(3) or (4) of
    the [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8),

  and for the purpose of this subsection, any provision of a law of a
  province that creates a deemed trust is, notwithstanding any Act of
  Canada or of a province or any other law, deemed to have the same
  effect and scope against any creditor, however secured, as the
  corresponding federal provision.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 67
- 1992, c. 27, s. 33
- 1996, c. 23, s. 168
- 1997, c. 12, s. 59
- 1998, c. 19, s. 250
- 2005, c. 47, s. 57
- 2007, c. 36, s. 32
- [2019, c. 29, s. 134](#1155878-1204931){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1155878-1204931.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-67-20080707.html "Link to previous version of section 67"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directives re surplus income

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Superintendent shall, by directive, establish in respect of the
  provinces or one or more bankruptcy districts or parts of bankruptcy
  districts, the standards for determining the surplus income of an
  individual bankrupt and the amount that a bankrupt who has surplus
  income is required to pay to the estate of the bankrupt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in this section.

  [[surplus income]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[surplus income]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the portion of a
      bankrupt individual's total income that exceeds that which is
      necessary to enable the bankrupt individual to maintain a
      reasonable standard of living, having regard to the applicable
      standards established under subsection (1). ([revenu
      excédentaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[total income]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[total income]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} includes, despite paragraphs 67(1)(b) and
        (b.3), a bankrupt's revenues of whatever nature or from whatever
        source that are earned or received by the bankrupt between the
        date of the bankruptcy and the date of the bankrupt's discharge,
        including those received as damages for wrongful dismissal,
        received as a pay equity settlement or received under an Act of
        Parliament, or of the legislature of a province, that relates to
        workers' compensation; but

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not include any amounts received by the
        bankrupt between the date of the bankruptcy and the date of the
        bankrupt's discharge, as a gift, a legacy or an inheritance or
        as any other windfall. ([revenu total]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of trustee re surplus
  income

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The trustee shall, having regard to the applicable
  standards and to the personal and family situation of the bankrupt,
  determine whether the bankrupt has surplus income. The determination
  must also be made

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whenever the trustee becomes aware of a material
    change in the bankrupt's financial situation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whenever the trustee is required to prepare a
    report referred to in subsection 170(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of trustee relating to
  determination

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Whenever the trustee is required to determine whether
  the bankrupt has surplus income, the trustee shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the trustee determines that there is surplus
    income,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} fix, having regard to the applicable standards,
      the amount that the bankrupt is required to pay to the estate of
      the bankrupt,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} inform, in the prescribed manner, the official
      receiver, and every creditor who has requested such information,
      of the amount fixed under subparagraph (i), and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} take reasonable measures to ensure that the
      bankrupt complies with the requirement to pay; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the trustee determines that there is no surplus
    income, inform, in the prescribed manner, the official receiver, and
    every creditor who has requested such information, of that
    determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official receiver recommendation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the official receiver determines that the amount
  required to be paid by the bankrupt is substantially not in accordance
  with the applicable standards, the official receiver shall recommend
  to the trustee and to the bankrupt an amount required to be paid that
  the official receiver determines is in accordance with the applicable
  standards.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee may fix another amount

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of the official receiver's
  recommendation, the trustee may fix, having regard to the applicable
  standards, another amount as the amount that the bankrupt is required
  to pay to the estate of the bankrupt, and if the trustee does so, the
  trustee shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} inform the official receiver and every creditor, in
    the prescribed manner, of the amount fixed under this subsection;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take reasonable measures to ensure that the
    bankrupt complies with the requirement to pay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee may request mediation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the trustee and the bankrupt are not in agreement
  with the amount that the bankrupt is required to pay under
  subsection (4) or (5.1), the trustee shall, without delay, in the
  prescribed form, send to the official receiver a request that the
  matter be determined by mediation and send a copy of the request to
  the bankrupt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Creditor may request mediation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On a creditor's request made within 30 days after the
  day on which the trustee informed the creditor of the amount fixed
  under subsection (4) or (5.1), the trustee shall, within five days
  after the day on which the 30-day period ends, send to the official
  receiver a request, in the prescribed form, that the matter of the
  amount that the bankrupt is required to pay be determined by mediation
  and send a copy of the request to the bankrupt and the creditor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mediation procedure

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A mediation shall be in accordance with prescribed
  procedures.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}File

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Documents contained in a file on the mediation of a
  matter under this section form part of the records referred to in
  subsection 11.1(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court to fix amount

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The trustee may, in any of the following
  circumstances --- and shall apply if requested to do so by the
  official receiver in the circumstances referred to in paragraph (a)
  --- apply to the court to fix, by order, in accordance with the
  applicable standards, and having regard to the personal and family
  situation of the bankrupt, the amount that the bankrupt is required to
  pay to the estate of the bankrupt:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the trustee has not implemented a recommendation
    made by the official receiver under subsection (5);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the matter submitted to mediation has not been
    resolved by the mediation; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the bankrupt has failed to comply with the
    requirement to pay as determined under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fixing fair and reasonable remuneration
  in the case of related persons

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The court may fix an amount that is fair and
  reasonable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as salary, wages or other remuneration for the
    services being performed by a bankrupt for a person employing the
    bankrupt, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as payment for or commission in respect of any
    services being performed by a bankrupt for a person,

  where the person is related to the bankrupt, and the court may, by
  order, determine the part of the salary, wages or other remuneration,
  or the part of the payment or commission, that shall be paid to the
  trustee on the basis of the amount so fixed by the court, unless it
  appears to the court that the services have been performed for the
  benefit of the bankrupt and are not of any substantial benefit to the
  person for whom they were performed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Modification of order

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} On the application of any interested person, the
  court may, at any time, amend an order made under this section to take
  into account material changes that have occurred in the financial
  situation of the bankrupt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Default by other person

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} An order of the court made under this section may be
  served on a person from whom the bankrupt is entitled to receive money
  and, in such case,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the order binds the person to pay to the estate of
    the bankrupt the amount fixed by the order; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the person fails to comply with the terms of the
    order, the court may, on the application of the trustee, order the
    person to pay the trustee the amount of money that the estate of the
    bankrupt would have received had the person complied with the terms
    of the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application is a proceeding

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of section 38, an application
  referred to in subsection (10) is deemed to be a proceeding for the
  benefit of the estate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property included for enforcement
  purposes

  [(15)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of this section, a requirement that
  a bankrupt pay an amount to the estate is enforceable against the
  bankrupt's total income.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When obligation to pay ceases

  [(16)]{.lawlabel} If an opposition to the automatic discharge of a
  bankrupt individual who is required to pay an amount to the estate is
  filed, the bankrupt's obligation under this section ceases on the day
  on which the bankrupt would have been automatically discharged had the
  opposition not been filed, but nothing in this subsection precludes
  the court from determining that the bankrupt is required to pay to the
  estate an amount that the court considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. B-3, s. 68
- 1992, c. 27, s. 34
- 1997, c. 12, s. 60
- 2005, c. 47, s. 58
- 2007, c. 36, s. 33
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-68-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 68"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment of wages

- **[[68.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  assignment of existing or future wages made by a debtor before the
  debtor became bankrupt is of no effect in respect of wages earned
  after the bankruptcy.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment of book debts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An assignment of existing or future amounts
  receivable as payment for or commission or professional fees in
  respect of services rendered by a debtor who is an individual before
  the debtor became bankrupt is of no effect in respect of such amounts
  earned or generated after the bankruptcy.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 27, s. 35
- 1997, c. 12, s. 61
- 2005, c. 47, s. 59
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-68.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 68.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Stay of Proceedings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-26356 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of proceedings --- notice of
intention

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3)
  and sections 69.4, 69.5 and 69.6, on the filing of a notice of
  intention under section 50.4 by an insolvent person,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} no creditor has any remedy against the insolvent
    person or the insolvent person's property, or shall commence or
    continue any action, execution or other proceedings, for the
    recovery of a claim provable in bankruptcy,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no provision of a security agreement between the
    insolvent person and a secured creditor that provides, in substance,
    that on

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the insolvent person's insolvency,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the default by the insolvent person of an
      obligation under the security agreement, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the filing by the insolvent person of a notice
      of intention under section 50.4,

    the insolvent person ceases to have such rights to use or deal with
    assets secured under the agreement as he would otherwise have, has
    any force or effect,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of Canada may not exercise Her
    rights under

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
      Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any provision of the [Canada Pension
      Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) or of the [Employment Insurance
      Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) that

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} refers to subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income
        Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} provides for the collection of a contribution,
        as defined in the [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8), an
        employee's premium or employer's premium, as defined in the
        [Employment Insurance Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6), or a premium under
        Part VII.1 of that Act, and of any related interest, penalties
        or other amounts,

    in respect of the insolvent person where the insolvent person is a
    tax debtor under that subsection or provision, and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of a province may not exercise
    her rights under any provision of provincial legislation in respect
    of the insolvent person where the insolvent person is a debtor under
    the provincial legislation and the provision has a similar purpose
    to subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or
    refers to that subsection, to the extent that it provides for the
    collection of a sum, and of any related interest, penalties or other
    amounts, where the sum

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has been withheld or deducted by a person from a
      payment to another person and is in respect of a tax similar in
      nature to the income tax imposed on individuals under the [Income
      Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is of the same nature as a contribution under
      the [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) if the province is a
      [[province providing a comprehensive pension
      plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in subsection 3(1) of the
      [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) and the provincial
      legislation establishes a [[provincial pension
      plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in that subsection,

  until the filing of a proposal under subsection 62(1) in respect of
  the insolvent person or the bankruptcy of the insolvent person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The stays provided by subsection (1) do not apply

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to prevent a secured creditor who took possession
    of secured assets of the insolvent person for the purpose of
    realization before the notice of intention under section 50.4 was
    filed from dealing with those assets;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to prevent a secured creditor who gave notice of
    intention under subsection 244(1) to enforce that creditor's
    security against the insolvent person more than ten days before the
    notice of intention under section 50.4 was filed, from enforcing
    that security, unless the secured creditor consents to the stay;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to prevent a secured creditor who gave notice of
    intention under subsection 244(1) to enforce that creditor's
    security from enforcing the security if the insolvent person has,
    under subsection 244(2), consented to the enforcement action; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 31, s. 416\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A stay provided by paragraph (1)(c) or (d) does not
  apply, or terminates, in respect of Her Majesty in right of Canada and
  every province if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the insolvent person defaults on payment of any
    amount that becomes due to Her Majesty after the filing of the
    notice of intention and could be subject to a demand under

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
      Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any provision of the [Canada Pension
      Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) or of the [Employment Insurance
      Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) that refers to subsection 224(1.2) of the
      [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) and provides for the collection
      of a contribution, as defined in the [Canada Pension
      Plan](/eng/acts/C-8), an employee's premium, or employer's
      premium, as defined in the [Employment Insurance
      Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6), or a premium under Part VII.1 of that Act,
      and of any related interest, penalties or other amounts, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any provision of provincial legislation that
      has a similar purpose to subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
      Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or that refers to that subsection, to the
      extent that it provides for the collection of a sum, and of any
      related interest, penalties or other amounts, where the sum

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} has been withheld or deducted by a person from
        a payment to another person and is in respect of a tax similar
        in nature to the income tax imposed on individuals under the
        [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} is of the same nature as a contribution under
        the [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) if the province is a
        [[province providing a comprehensive pension
        plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in subsection 3(1) of the
        [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) and the provincial
        legislation establishes a [[provincial pension
        plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in that subsection; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other creditor is or becomes entitled to
    realize a security on any property that could be claimed by Her
    Majesty in exercising Her rights under

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
      Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any provision of the [Canada Pension
      Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) or of the [Employment Insurance
      Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) that refers to subsection 224(1.2) of the
      [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) and provides for the collection
      of a contribution, as defined in the [Canada Pension
      Plan](/eng/acts/C-8), an employee's premium, or employer's
      premium, as defined in the [Employment Insurance
      Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6), or a premium under Part VII.1 of that Act,
      and of any related interest, penalties or other amounts, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any provision of provincial legislation that
      has a similar purpose to subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
      Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or that refers to that subsection, to the
      extent that it provides for the collection of a sum, and of any
      related interest, penalties or other amounts, where the sum

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} has been withheld or deducted by a person from
        a payment to another person and is in respect of a tax similar
        in nature to the income tax imposed on individuals under the
        [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} is of the same nature as a contribution under
        the [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) if the province is a
        [[province providing a comprehensive pension
        plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in subsection 3(1) of the
        [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) and the provincial
        legislation establishes a [[provincial pension
        plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in that subsection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of proceedings --- Division I
proposals

- **[[69.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (6)
  and sections 69.4, 69.5 and 69.6, on the filing of a proposal under
  subsection 62(1) in respect of an insolvent person,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} no creditor has any remedy against the insolvent
    person or the insolvent person's property, or shall commence or
    continue any action, execution or other proceedings, for the
    recovery of a claim provable in bankruptcy, until the trustee has
    been discharged or the insolvent person becomes bankrupt;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no provision of a security agreement between the
    insolvent person and a secured creditor that provides, in substance,
    that on

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the insolvent person's insolvency,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the default by the insolvent person of an
      obligation under the security agreement, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the filing of a notice of intention under
      section 50.4 or of a proposal under subsection 62(1) in respect of
      the insolvent person,

    the insolvent person ceases to have such rights to use or deal with
    assets secured under the agreement as the insolvent person would
    otherwise have, has any force or effect until the trustee has been
    discharged or the insolvent person becomes bankrupt;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of Canada may not exercise Her
    rights under subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
    Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) or any provision of the [Canada Pension
    Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) or of the [Employment Insurance
    Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) that refers to subsection 224(1.2) of the
    [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) and provides for the collection of
    a contribution, as defined in the [Canada Pension
    Plan](/eng/acts/C-8), an employee's premium, or employer's premium,
    as defined in the [Employment Insurance Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6), or a
    premium under Part VII.1 of that Act, and of any related interest,
    penalties or other amounts, in respect of the insolvent person where
    the insolvent person is a tax debtor under that subsection or
    provision, until

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the trustee has been discharged,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} six months have elapsed following court approval
      of the proposal, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the insolvent person becomes bankrupt; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of a province may not exercise
    Her rights under any provision of provincial legislation that has a
    similar purpose to subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
    Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or that refers to that subsection, to the
    extent that it provides for the collection of a sum, and of any
    related interest, penalties or other amounts, where the sum

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has been withheld or deducted by a person from a
      payment to another person and is in respect of a tax similar in
      nature to the income tax imposed on individuals under the [Income
      Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is of the same nature as a contribution under
      the [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) if the province is a
      [[province providing a comprehensive pension
      plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in subsection 3(1) of the
      [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) and the provincial
      legislation establishes a [[provincial pension
      plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in that subsection,

    in respect of the insolvent person where the insolvent person is a
    debtor under the provincial legislation, until

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the trustee has been discharged,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} six months have elapsed following court approval
      of the proposal, or

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the insolvent person becomes bankrupt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The stays provided by subsection (1) do not apply

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to prevent a secured creditor who took possession
    of secured assets of the insolvent person for the purpose of
    realization before the proposal was filed from dealing with those
    assets;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} unless the secured creditor otherwise agrees, to
    prevent a secured creditor who gave notice of intention under
    subsection 244(1) to enforce that creditor's security against the
    insolvent person more than ten days before

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a notice of intention was filed in respect of the
      insolvent person under section 50.4, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the proposal was filed, if no notice of
      intention under section 50.4 was filed

    from enforcing that security;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to prevent a secured creditor who gave notice of
    intention under subsection 244(1) to enforce that creditor's
    security from enforcing the security if the insolvent person has,
    under subsection 244(2), consented to the enforcement action; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 31, s. 417\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A stay provided by paragraph (1)(c) or (d) does not
  apply, or terminates, in respect of Her Majesty in right of Canada and
  every province if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the insolvent person defaults on payment of any
    amount that becomes due to Her Majesty after the filing of the
    proposal and could be subject to a demand under

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
      Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any provision of the [Canada Pension
      Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) or of the [Employment Insurance
      Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) that refers to subsection 224(1.2) of the
      [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) and provides for the collection
      of a contribution, as defined in the [Canada Pension
      Plan](/eng/acts/C-8), an employee's premium, or employer's
      premium, as defined in the [Employment Insurance
      Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6), or a premium under Part VII.1 of that Act,
      and of any related interest, penalties or other amounts, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any provision of provincial legislation that
      has a similar purpose to subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
      Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or that refers to that subsection, to the
      extent that it provides for the collection of a sum, and of any
      related interest, penalties or other amounts, where the sum

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} has been withheld or deducted by a person from
        a payment to another person and is in respect of a tax similar
        in nature to the income tax imposed on individuals under the
        [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} is of the same nature as a contribution under
        the [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) if the province is a
        [[province providing a comprehensive pension
        plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in subsection 3(1) of the
        [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) and the provincial
        legislation establishes a [[provincial pension
        plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in that subsection; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other creditor is or becomes entitled to
    realize a security on any property that could be claimed by Her
    Majesty in exercising Her rights under

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
      Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any provision of the [Canada Pension
      Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) or of the [Employment Insurance
      Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) that refers to subsection 224(1.2) of the
      [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) and provides for the collection
      of a contribution, as defined in the [Canada Pension
      Plan](/eng/acts/C-8), an employee's premium, or employer's
      premium, as defined in the [Employment Insurance
      Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6), or a premium under Part VII.1 of that Act,
      and of any related interest, penalties or other amounts, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any provision of provincial legislation that
      has a similar purpose to subsection 224(1.2) of the [Income Tax
      Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or that refers to that subsection, to the
      extent that it provides for the collection of a sum, and of any
      related interest, penalties or other amounts, where the sum

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} has been withheld or deducted by a person from
        a payment to another person and is in respect of a tax similar
        in nature to the income tax imposed on individuals under the
        [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} is of the same nature as a contribution under
        the [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) if the province is a
        [[province providing a comprehensive pension
        plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in subsection 3(1) of the
        [Canada Pension Plan](/eng/acts/C-8) and the provincial
        legislation establishes a [[provincial pension
        plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} as defined in that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, by virtue of subsection 69(3), the stay provided
  by paragraph 69(1)(c) or (d) does not apply or terminates, the stay
  provided by paragraph (1)(c) or (d) of this section does not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Secured creditors to whom proposal not
  made

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 79 and 127 to 135 and subsection
  248(1), the filing of a proposal under subsection 62(1) does not
  prevent a secured creditor to whom the proposal has not been made in
  respect of a particular security from realizing or otherwise dealing
  with that security in the same manner as he would have been entitled
  to realize or deal with it if this section had not been passed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where secured creditors vote against
  proposal

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 79 and 127 to 135 and subsection
  248(1), where secured creditors holding a particular class of secured
  claim vote for the refusal of a proposal, a secured creditor holding a
  secured claim of that class may henceforth realize or otherwise deal
  with his security in the same manner as he would have been entitled to
  realize or deal with it if this section had not been passed.
